Q1. One worker can do a job in 12 days and another can do it in 24 days. In how many days will they complete it together?
A. 36 days
B. 12 days
C. 8 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(12,24)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/12=2,\quad B=24/24=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{2+1}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{12\times 24}{12+24}=8
\]
Answer: 8 days

---

Q2. The efficiency ratio A:B is 4:7, while B:C is 7:8. Find the ratio A:B:C?
A. 4:7:8
B. 8:7:4
C. 4:7:8:2
D. 4:7:8:3
Answer: 4:7:8
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_efficiency_chain
Explanation:
Work with rates, not with completion times directly.
\[
B_c
\]
Use the given pair, team, or contribution data to isolate the required rate.
\[
LCM(7,7)=7
\]
Convert the isolated rate or values into the asked result.
\[
A:B:C=4:7:8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A:B:C=4:7:8
\]
Answer: 4:7:8

---

Q3. A takes 10 days to finish a work and B takes 30 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?
A. 7 1/2 days
B. 10 days
C. 40 days
D. 20 days
Answer: 7 1/2 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(10,30)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/10=3,\quad B=30/30=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{3+1}=7.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{10\times 30}{10+30}=7.5
\]
Answer: 7 1/2 days

---

Q4. A and B's efficiencies are 12:24. What is the ratio of days required by A and B separately?
A. 2:1
B. 36:24
C. 12:24
D. 24:36
Answer: 2:1
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_time_ratio_from_efficiency
Explanation:
Convert time or efficiency data into work rates.
\[
T_A:T_B=E_B:E_A
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
T_A:T_B=24:12
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
T_A:T_B=2:1
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2:1
\]
Answer: 2:1

---

Q5. 18 workers finish a job in 8 days, working 5 hours daily. How many workers should work 6 hours daily to finish it in 12 days?
A. 27 workers
B. 10 workers
C. 13 workers
D. 18 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_farm_harvest_workers
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
18\times 8\times 5=M_2\times 12\times 6
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{18\times 8\times 5}{12\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

---

Q6. A completes 8 metres whenever he works and then rests the next day. How many days will 120 metres take?
A. 36 days
B. 37 1/2 days
C. 28 days
D. 30 days
Answer: 30 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_work_rest_cycle
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=8+0
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 30 days

---

Q7. A team of 8 workers has completed 48 items from a 360-item order. With 12 days left, how many workers must be added?
A. 18 workers
B. 13.5 workers
C. 16 workers
D. 13 workers
Answer: 18 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{312}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-8
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 18 workers

---

Q8. A worker finishes \(\frac{4}{9}\) of a work in 6 days. How many days are needed for the full work?
A. 54 days
B. 13 1/2 days
C. 2 2/3 days
D. 15 days
Answer: 13 1/2 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_fraction_days
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
T=6\times \frac{9}{4}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
T=13.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=6\div \frac{4}{9}=13.5
\]
Answer: 13 1/2 days

---

Q9. A can finish \(\frac{3}{8}\) of a work in 8 days. In how many days can A finish all of it?
A. 64 days
B. 3 days
C. 16 days
D. 21 1/3 days
Answer: 21 1/3 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_partial_completion_then_team
Explanation:

\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
T=8\times \frac{8}{3}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
T=21\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=8\div \frac{3}{8}=21\frac{1}{3}
\]
Answer: 21 1/3 days

Q10. A can do a work in 20 days. With B, the same work is completed in 12 days. In how many days can B alone complete it?
A. 8 days
B. 32 days
C. 30 days
D. 16 days
Answer: 30 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_individual_from_combined
Explanation:
Convert time or efficiency data into work rates.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
B=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{12\times 20}{20-12}=30
\]
Answer: 30 days

---

Q11. One-fourth of a tank is filled. The pipe can fill a full tank in 12 hours. In how many hours will the remaining part be filled?
A. 14 hours
B. 12 hours
C. 10 4/5 hours
D. 9 hours
Answer: 9 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_partial_tank_initially_filled
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{1-f}{r_f}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
T=\frac{1-1/4}{1/12}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=(1-f)F
\]
Answer: 9 hours

---

Q12. For 4 days a team completes 6 forms per day. Then the team changes and completes 15 forms per day. How many days will 60 forms take altogether?
A. 8 days
B. 5 2/5 days
C. 1 2/5 days
D. 6 2/5 days
Answer: 6 2/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_multi_phase_join_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
\sum r_it_i=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=6\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{36}{15}=6.4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 6 2/5 days

---

\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=10+6
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=14.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 14 4/5 days

Q14. For equal working time, A is paid ₹800 and B is paid ₹1200. What is the ratio of their work?
A. 2000:1200
B. 2:3
C. 1200:800
D. 80:60
Answer: 2:3
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_efficiency_from_wages
Explanation:
Work with rates, not with completion times directly.
\[
W_A:W_B=E_A:E_B
\]
Use the given pair, team, or contribution data to isolate the required rate.
\[
E_A:E_B=800:1200
\]
Convert the isolated rate or values into the asked result.
\[
E_A:E_B=2:3
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=2:3
\]
Answer: 2:3

Q15. 30 people have food for 24 days. After 8 days, there are 40 people. For how many additional days will the food be sufficient?
A. 16 days
B. 12 days
C. 11 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_population_change
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720-30\times 8}{40}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 12 days

---

Q18. A, B and C divide ₹7200 according to the work-share ratio 30:30:12. What will C receive?
A. ₹1500
B. ₹1195
C. ₹1200
D. ₹1202
Answer: ₹1200
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_wage_with_helper
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
Share=\frac{c_i}{\sum c}\times W
\]
Use the work-share ratio for the wage.
\[
Share=\frac{12}{72}\times 7200
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
Share=1200
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
Share=contribution\ ratio\times wage
\]
Answer: ₹1200

---

Q19. 3 men, 4 women work together on 240 boxes. Their daily capacities are 4, 3 boxes respectively. How many days will they take?
A. 10 days
B. 13 days
C. 15 days
D. 6 days
Answer: 10 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_team_a_vs_team_b
Explanation:
Convert every worker type into a common efficiency unit.
\[
T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}
\]
Add the equivalent units in the working team.
\[
T=\frac{240}{3\times 4+4\times 3}
\]
Divide the total work by the team's equivalent rate.
\[
T=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 10 days

---

Q21. A and B start a work together. A can complete it in 12 days and B in 18 days. After 5 days, A leaves. In how many days will the work be completed in all?
A. 10 1/2 days
B. 11 1/2 days
C. 6 1/2 days
D. 15 3/4 days
Answer: 10 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(3+2)\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{11}{2}=10.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=5+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 10 1/2 days

---

Q22. The efficiency ratio of A to B is 2:3. They complete the work together in 6 days. In how many days can A do it alone?
A. 4 days
B. 9 days
C. 15 days
D. 11 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_relative_efficiency_percent
Explanation:
Convert time or efficiency data into work rates.
\[
W=(a+b)T
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
W=(2+3)\times 6
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
T_A=\frac{30}{2}=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(2+3)6}{2}=15
\]
Answer: 15 days

Q24. A factory uses three machines. Machine A makes 12 parts per hour, B makes 15, and C makes 18. How many parts are produced in 6 hours?
A. 265 items
B. 337.5 items
C. 270 items
D. 267 items
Answer: 270 items
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_machine_breakdown_midway
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
O=\sum r_it_i
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
O=12\times 6+15\times 6+18\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
O=270
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O=(r_1+r_2+r_3)t
\]
Answer: 270 items

---

Q25. A can complete a work in 12 days and B can complete it in 24 days. A starts alone, and after 3 days B joins him. In how many days will the whole work be completed?
A. 9 days
B. 14 days
C. 10 4/5 days
D. 7 days
Answer: 9 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=10\times 3,\quad R=120-30
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{90}{10+5}=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{120-10\times 3}{10+5}
\]
Answer: 9 days

---

Q26. Pipe A fills a tank in 12 hours. After 2 hours, a leak begins which empties a full tank in 30 hours. Find the total time to fill the tank?
A. 20 2/3 hours
B. 28 hours
C. 18 2/3 hours
D. 22 2/3 hours
Answer: 18 2/3 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_leak_starts_after_fill
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_f-r_e}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
r=1/12-1/30
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=18\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 18 2/3 hours

Q28. A team repairs 15 metres daily, but another effect damages 3 metres daily. How many days are needed to complete 120 metres?
A. 7 days
B. 5 days
C. 12 1/2 days
D. 10 days
Answer: 10 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_negative_positive_mixed_work
Explanation:
Choose work units so that all rates stay comparable.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Apply the active rates for their respective times.
\[
T=\frac{120}{15-3}
\]
Use the resulting work equation to compute the asked quantity.
\[
T=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 10 days

Answer: 10 days

Family: tw_phase_state_reconstruction
Explanation:

\[
x=\frac{W-W_1}{r}

\[
x=\frac{120-60}{6}
\]

x=10
\]

\[
x=\frac{W_l}{r}
\]

Q33. A team takes 15 days for a job. After working for 10 days, what fraction of the job remains?
A. 3/1
B. 2/3
C. 1/2
D. 1/3
Answer: 1/3
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_residual_work
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{10}{15}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{5}{15}=1/3
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{15-10}{15}=1/3
\]
Answer: 1/3

Q37. A team of 12 workers works for 6 days, then 4 workers leave. What is the delay in completion?
A. 15 days
B. 12 days
C. 17 days
D. 14 2/5 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_worker_removed_delay
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
D=T_{actual}-T_{planned}
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
R=360-12\times 6
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
D=\frac{288}{8}-\frac{288}{12}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=T_a-T_p
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q38. A repairs 6 metres of road on his turn and B repairs 4 metres on the next turn. They continue alternately, starting with A. If the road length is 60 metres, in how many days will the repair be completed?

Q40. A group is expected to complete a work in 30 days. It has already worked for 8 days. What fraction remains?

Q47. A job is finished by 12 workers in 10 days when each works 8 hours daily. How many workers are needed for 16 days at 6 hours daily?
A. 13 workers
B. 12 workers
C. 19 workers
D. 10 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
12\times 10\times 8=M_2\times 16\times 6
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{12\times 10\times 8}{16\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q49. A and B can separately complete a work in 8 and 6 days. A is absent for the last 2 days and B is absent for the last 3 days. In how many days is the work completed?
A. 9 days
B. 6 days
C. 11 days
D. 7 1/5 days
Answer: 6 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the total work as the sum of phase-wise work.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Use each phase rate only for the time it is active.
\[
3(T-2)+4(T-3)=24
\]
Solve the phase equation for the required time.
\[
T=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 6 days

Q53. A, B and C together complete a work in 6 days. A and B together can complete it in 12 days. In how many days will C alone complete the work?

Explanation:

\[
r_X=\frac{1}{6}-\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{24}
\]
Convert the isolated rate or values into the asked result.

T_X=24
\]

\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]

Q54. Three pairs of workers finish the same job in 10, 12 and 15 days. If the pairs are AB, BC and CA, in how many days can A alone finish it?
A. 21 days
B. 18 days
C. 29 days
D. 24 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_pairwise_ab_bc_ac
Explanation:

\[
2(A+B+C)=(A+B)+(B+C)+(C+A)
\]
Use the given pair, team, or contribution data to isolate the required rate.
\[
AB=10,\ BC=12,\ CA=15
\]
Convert the isolated rate or values into the asked result.
\[
T_A=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A=\frac{AB+AC-BC}{2}
\]
Answer: 24 days

Q55. A and B complete the same work separately in 10 and 15 days. What is the ratio of work done by A and B in one day?

Explanation:

\[
W_A:W_B=\frac{1}{10}:\frac{1}{15}
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
W_A:W_B=15:10
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
W_A:W_B=3:2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=3:2
\]
Answer: 3:2

Q56. Pipe A takes 12 hours and pipe B takes 18 hours to fill the tank alone. If Pipe B is opened 2 hours later, in how many hours will the tank be completely filled?
A. 6 hours
B. 8 hours
C. 3 hours
D. 9 hours
Answer: 8 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_multiple_pipes_timing
Explanation:

\[

\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=3\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{30}{3+2}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Answer: 8 hours

Q57. A and B have efficiencies in the ratio 3:4. If they finish a job together in 12 days, how many days will A alone take?

Explanation:

\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
T_A=\frac{84}{3}=28
\]

\[

\]
Answer: 28 days

Q73. The efficiencies of A and B are in the ratio 2:3. Together they complete a work in 6 days. In how many days can A alone complete the work?

B. 4 days

D. 11 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_efficiency_ratio_scaling
Explanation:

\]

\[
W=(2+3)\times 6
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
T_A=\frac{30}{2}=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(2+3)6}{2}=15
\]
Answer: 15 days

Q75. After a delay, 120 of 300 records are completed by 12 workers. How many extra workers are needed if the remaining records must be finished in 10 days?

B. 9 workers
C. 5 workers
D. 6 workers
Answer: 6 workers
Difficulty: Hard

Explanation:

\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{180}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-12
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 6 workers

Q76. 18 workers working 5 hours per day finish the job in 8 days. If the daily working time is 6 hours, how many workers are needed to finish it in 12 days?

Q77. Pipe A fills a tank in 12 hours. A drain pipe can empty the tank in 36 hours. If both are open, in how many hours will the tank be completely filled?

D. 24 hours
Answer: 24 hours
Difficulty: Medium

\[

\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{12}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 24 hours

T=\frac{10\times 15}{10+15}=6
\]
Answer: 6 days

Write the total work as the sum of phase-wise work.
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-r_1t_1}{r_2}
\]
Use each phase rate only for the time it is active.
\[
T=4+\frac{180-15\times 4}{12}
\]
Solve the phase equation for the required time.
\[
T=14
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+t_2
\]
Answer: 14 days

---

Q110. In a workshop, each man finishes 4 boxes daily and each woman finishes 2 boxes daily. In how many days will 2 men and 3 women finish 240 boxes?
A. 16 1/3 days
B. 20 days
C. 13 1/3 days
D. 9 1/3 days
Answer: 13 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_equivalence_men_women_children
Explanation:
Convert every worker type into a common efficiency unit.
\[
T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}
\]
Add the equivalent units in the working team.
\[
T=\frac{240}{2\times 4+3\times 2+4\times 1}
\]
Divide the total work by the team's equivalent rate.
\[
T=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

Q113. Three workers receive ₹9000 for a job. Their contributions are proportional to 24:36:40. How much will A get?

Q114. A job can be finished in 20 days. If work continues for 8 days, what fraction is still incomplete?
A. 5/3
B. 2/5
C. 3/5
D. 3/2
Answer: 3/5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{8}{20}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{12}{20}=3/5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{20-8}{20}=3/5
\]
Answer: 3/5

Q115. A job would be completed in 12 days of work. Due to a 3 days stoppage, in how many calendar days is it completed?
A. 15 days
B. 14 days
C. 18 3/4 days
D. 10 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{276}{8+15}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+3=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 15 days

Q116. A tank is filled by a pipe in 10 hours. When a leak is present, it takes 20 hours. How many hours will the leak alone take to empty the tank?
A. 20 hours
B. 15 hours
C. 25 hours
D. 19 hours
Answer: 20 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 20 hours

Q122. 15 workers complete the same job in 16 days at 6 hours a day. How many workers are required to complete the same job in 12 days at 8 hours a day?
A. 15 workers
B. 18 workers
C. 20 workers
D. 11 workers
Answer: 15 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
15\times 16\times 6=M_2\times 12\times 8
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
M_2=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{15\times 16\times 6}{12\times 8}=15
\]
Answer: 15 workers

Family: tw_efficiency_from_wages

Work with rates, not with completion times directly.
\[
W_A:W_B=E_A:E_B
\]
Use the given pair, team, or contribution data to isolate the required rate.
\[
E_A:E_B=450:600
\]
Convert the isolated rate or values into the asked result.
\[
E_A:E_B=3:4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=3:4
\]
Answer: 3:4

---

Q146. Food for 24 people lasts 25 days. If after 8 days the number of people changes to 34, how many more days will it last?
A. 7 days
B. 15 days
C. 11 days
D. 12 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_population_change
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{600-24\times 8}{34}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q150. A team has 3 men and 4 women. They finish 4, 3 boxes per person per day respectively. How many days are needed for 360 boxes?
A. 15 days
B. 21 days
C. 12 days
D. 7 1/2 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_team_a_vs_team_b
Explanation:

\[
T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}
\]
Add the equivalent units in the working team.
\[
T=\frac{360}{3\times 4+4\times 3}
\]
Divide the total work by the team's equivalent rate.
\[
T=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 15 days

Q151. A repairs 6 metres of road on his turn and B repairs 4 metres on the next turn. They continue alternately, starting with A. If the road length is 60 metres, in how many hours will the repair be completed?
A. 15 hours
B. 12 hours
C. 9 hours
D. 7 hours
Answer: 12 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_hours
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=6+4
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 hours

Q152. A can finish a work in 10 days and B in 16 days. They work together for 5 days, then B alone finishes it. How many days are taken altogether?
A. 13 days
B. 9 3/5 days
C. 8 days
D. 9 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(8+5)\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{15}{5}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=5+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 8 days

Q153. The efficiencies of A and B are in the ratio 4:5. Together they complete a work in 10 days. In how many days can A alone complete the work?

B. 8 days
C. 12 1/2 days
D. 19 days
Answer: 22 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_relative_efficiency_percent
Explanation:
Convert time or efficiency data into work rates.
\[
W=(a+b)T
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
W=(4+5)\times 10
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
T_A=\frac{90}{4}=22.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(4+5)10}{4}=22.5
\]
Answer: 22 1/2 days

Q181. Normally a pipe fills a tank in 12 hours, but with leakage it fills in 18 hours. What is the emptying time of the leak?
A. 39 hours
B. 27 hours
C. 31 hours
D. 36 hours
Answer: 36 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{18}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=36
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 36 hours

Q182. 18 workers complete the same job in 8 days at 5 hours a day. How many workers are required to complete the same job in 12 days at 6 hours a day?
A. 27 workers
B. 18 workers
C. 13 workers
D. 10 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
18\times 8\times 5=M_2\times 12\times 6
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{18\times 8\times 5}{12\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q184. In a joint job, A alone needs 10 days and B alone needs 20 days. A is not present in the last 2 days and B in the last 3 days. How many days does the job take?
A. 7 days
B. 13 1/2 days
C. 5 days
D. 9 days
Answer: 9 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the total work as the sum of phase-wise work.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Use each phase rate only for the time it is active.
\[
26(T-2)+13(T-3)=260
\]
Solve the phase equation for the required time.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 9 days

Q191. One pipe fills a tank in 12 hours, while another empties it in 36 hours. In how many hours will the tank be completely filled with both open?
A. 30 hours
B. 36 hours
C. 35 hours
D. 31 hours
Answer: 30 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:

\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{15}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 30 hours

Q192. A can complete a piece of work in 15 days and B can complete the same work in 30 days. In how many days will they complete it working together?

Explanation:

\[

\]

\[

\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{2+1}=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{15\times 30}{15+30}=10
\]
Answer: 10 days

Family: tw_worker_removed_delay
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
D=T_{actual}-T_{planned}
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
R=240-10\times 10
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
D=\frac{140}{6}-\frac{140}{10}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=T_a-T_p
\]
Answer: 9 1/3 days

Q219. Two workers take turns, with A starting. A prints 8 pages per turn and B prints 4 pages per turn. In how many days will 72 pages be printed?

Q220. A group is expected to complete a work in 30 days. It has already worked for 3 days. What fraction remains?
A. 9/1
B. 1/10
C. 10/9
D. 9/10
Answer: 9/10
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:

\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{3}{30}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{27}{30}=9/10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{30-3}{30}=9/10
\]
Answer: 9/10

Q221. A group needs 12 working days to complete a job. If there is a break of 3 days, how many days pass from start to finish?
A. 17 days
B. 18 days
C. 11 days
D. 15 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{384}{20+12}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+3=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 15 days

Q222. A tank is filled by a pipe in 12 hours. When a leak is present, it takes 22 hours. How many hours will the leak alone take to empty the tank?
A. 28 2/5 hours
B. 30 2/5 hours
C. 39 3/5 hours
D. 26 2/5 hours
Answer: 26 2/5 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{22}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=26.4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 26 2/5 hours

Q223. 10 workers can complete a job in 15 days by working 8 hours a day. How many workers are needed to finish it in 20 days by working 6 hours a day?
A. 14 workers
B. 15 workers
C. 13 workers
D. 10 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
10\times 15\times 8=M_2\times 20\times 6
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{10\times 15\times 8}{20\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q225. A alone takes 10 days and B alone takes 15 days. During a joint job, A does not work in the last 2 days and B does not work in the last 3 days. What is the total time?
A. 10 2/5 days
B. 10 1/2 days
C. 3 2/5 days
D. 8 2/5 days
Answer: 8 2/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the total work as the sum of phase-wise work.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Use each phase rate only for the time it is active.
\[
6(T-2)+4(T-3)=60
\]
Solve the phase equation for the required time.
\[
T=8.4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 8 2/5 days

Q232. A takes 20 days to finish a work and B takes 12 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?

B. 32 days
C. 7 1/2 days
D. 12 days
Answer: 7 1/2 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:

\[
W=LCM(20,12)=60
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=60/20=3,\quad B=60/12=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{60}{3+5}=7.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{20\times 12}{20+12}=7.5
\]
Answer: 7 1/2 days

Q253. 30 people have food for 30 days. After 8 days, there are 40 people. For how many additional days will the food be sufficient?
A. 15 1/2 days
B. 24 3/4 days
C. 20 1/2 days
D. 16 1/2 days
Answer: 16 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_population_change
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{900-30\times 8}{40}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=16.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]

Q256. A and B jointly work for 4 days. A then leaves, and B completes the job. If A and B alone need 8 and 12 days, how many days does the work take?

\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2

\[

\[

\]

Q257. A alone takes 8 days and B alone takes 24 days. A works alone for 2 days, then B joins. How many days will the job take in all?

Q261. A job needs 15 days to finish. After 2 days of work, what part of the job is still left?
A. 2/15
B. 15/13
C. 13/2
D. 13/15
Answer: 13/15
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_residual_work
Explanation:

\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{2}{15}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{13}{15}=13/15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{15-2}{15}=13/15
\]
Answer: 13/15

Q267. A pipe fills a tank in 10 hours. A leak slows the filling to 16 hours. What time will the leak take to empty the tank alone?

D. 20 hours
Answer: 26 2/3 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{16}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=26\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 26 2/3 hours

W_A:W_B=3:1
\]

E=3:1
\]

Q273. A team of 12 workers has completed 72 items from a 360-item order. With 12 days left, how many workers must be added?

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Q274. A takes 20 days to finish a work and B takes 30 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?
A. 25 days
B. 50 days
C. 12 days
D. 20 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(20,30)=60
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=60/20=3,\quad B=60/30=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{60}{3+2}=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{20\times 30}{20+30}=12
\]
Answer: 12 days

Write the total work as the sum of phase-wise work.
\[
x=\frac{W-W_1}{r}
\]
Use each phase rate only for the time it is active.
\[
x=\frac{120-48}{10}
\]
Solve the phase equation for the required time.
\[
x=7.2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
x=\frac{W_l}{r}
\]
Answer: 7 1/5 days

---

Q291. A job needs 18 days to finish. After 5 days of work, what part of the job is still left?
A. 18/13
B. 13/5
C. 5/18
D. 13/18
Answer: 13/18
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_residual_work
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{5}{18}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{13}{18}=13/18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{18-5}{18}=13/18
\]
Answer: 13/18

Q292. 30 people have food for 36 days. If the number of people becomes 40, for how many days will the food last?

B. 32 days
C. 27 days
D. 30 days
Answer: 27 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:

\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
30\times 36=40\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=27
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{30\times 36}{40}
\]
Answer: 27 days

Q297. Without leakage, a tank fills in 10 hours. With leakage, it fills in 15 hours. Find the leak's emptying time in hours?
A. 27 hours
B. 36 hours
C. 24 hours
D. 30 hours
Answer: 30 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{15}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 30 hours

\]

\]

Q309. 10 workers have packed 100 boxes out of 360. To finish the rest in 12 days, how many additional workers are needed?

\[

\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{260}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-10
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 12 workers

Q310. One worker can do a job in 6 days and another can do it in 30 days. In how many days will they complete it together?
A. 6 days
B. 18 days
C. 36 days
D. 5 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:

\[
W=LCM(6,30)=30
\]

\[
A=30/6=5,\quad B=30/30=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{5+1}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{6\times 30}{6+30}=5
\]
Answer: 5 days

A. 39 1/2 hours
B. 56 1/4 hours
C. 37 1/2 hours
D. 41 1/2 hours
Answer: 37 1/2 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{15}-\frac{1}{25}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=37.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 37 1/2 hours

---

Q328. A, B and C work on successive days in that order. They pack 8, 4 and 5 boxes respectively on their turns. If 72 boxes are to be packed, in how many days will the job be completed?
A. 12 1/2 days
B. 15 days
C. 17 1/2 days
D. 15 1/2 days
Answer: 12 1/2 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_conditional_activation
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=8+4+5
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 1/2 days

Q329. A team adds 15 metres to a project each day, but 3 metres are lost each day. What is the time for 150 metres?
A. 10 1/2 days
B. 15 days
C. 17 1/2 days
D. 12 1/2 days
Answer: 12 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_digging_filling_negative_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
T=\frac{150}{15-3}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=12.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 12 1/2 days

Q330. A finishes a job in 8 days and B in 12 days. What is the ratio of work done by A and B in the same time?
A. 3:2
B. 12:20
C. 8:12
D. 20:12
Answer: 3:2
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_done_ratio_from_times
Explanation:
Convert time or efficiency data into work rates.
\[
W_A:W_B=\frac{1}{8}:\frac{1}{12}
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
W_A:W_B=12:8
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
W_A:W_B=3:2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=3:2
\]
Answer: 3:2

Q331. 12 workers have packed 96 boxes out of 240. To finish the rest in 8 days, how many additional workers are needed?
A. 1 worker
B. 9 workers
C. 4.8 workers
D. 6 workers
Answer: 6 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_contract_deadline_extra_workers
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{144}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-12
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 6 workers

---

Q332. A can complete a piece of work in 18 days and B can complete the same work in 6 days. In how many days will they complete it working together?
A. 12 days
B. 6 days
C. 24 days
D. 4 1/2 days
Answer: 4 1/2 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(18,6)=18
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=18/18=1,\quad B=18/6=3
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{18}{1+3}=4.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{18\times 6}{18+6}=4.5
\]
Answer: 4 1/2 days

\]

\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A:B:C=3:5:6
\]
Answer: 3:5:6

\]

Q340. A camp has food for 24 people for 25 days. After 6 days, the camp has 30 people. For how many more days will the remaining food last?

C. 15 1/5 days
D. 13 1/5 days
Answer: 15 1/5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_population_change
Explanation:

\[

\]

\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=15.2
\]

\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 15 1/5 days

Q342. A and B jointly work for 2 days. A then leaves, and B completes the job. If A and B alone need 30 and 18 days, how many days does the work take?

C. 12 3/5 days
D. 21 4/5 days
Answer: 16 4/5 days

Family: tw_forward_leave

\[

\]

\[

\]

T=2+\frac{296}{20}=16.8
\]

\[
T=2+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]

Q343. A starts a work and B joins after 2 days. A and B can finish the work alone in 12 and 6 days. What is the total time taken?

Answer: 5 1/3 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join

\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[

\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{50}{5+10}=5\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2+\frac{60-5\times 2}{5+10}
\]
Answer: 5 1/3 days

Q363. A can complete a piece of work in 12 days and B can complete the same work in 18 days. In how many days will they complete it working together?
A. 15 days
B. 7 1/5 days
C. 30 days
D. 12 days
Answer: 7 1/5 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(12,18)=36
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=36/12=3,\quad B=36/18=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{36}{3+2}=7.2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{12\times 18}{12+18}=7.2
\]
Answer: 7 1/5 days

Q366. 12 workers have packed 72 boxes out of 240. To finish the rest in 10 days, how many additional workers are needed?

B. 3 workers
C. 6 workers
D. 10 workers
Answer: 5 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{168}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-12
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 5 workers

Q367. A completes \(\frac{1}{3}\) of a job in 9 days. In how many days will A complete the whole job?

\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]

\[
T=9\times \frac{3}{1}
\]

T=27
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=9\div \frac{1}{3}=27
\]
Answer: 27 days

Q369. Food is sufficient for 30 people for 20 days. After 8 days, the number of people becomes 36. For how many more days will the food last?
A. 10 days
B. 15 days
C. 6 days
D. 13 days
Answer: 10 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_population_change
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{600-30\times 8}{36}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 10 days

Q371. A and B work together at first. A's time is 12 days and B's time is 6 days. If A leaves after 2 days, when will the job be finished in all?
A. 4 days
B. 5 days
C. 7 days
D. 11 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(5+10)\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{30}{10}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 5 days

Q375. A begins a job alone. A can finish it in 10 days and B can finish it in 20 days. If B joins after 3 days, how many days will the job take in all?

D. 7 2/3 days
Answer: 7 2/3 days
Difficulty: Medium

Explanation:

\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]

\[
W_1=26\times 3,\quad R=260-78
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{182}{26+13}=7\frac{2}{3}

\]
Answer: 7 2/3 days

Q376. A road crew lays 18 metres of road per day, while rain damages 3 metres per day. If 150 metres must be completed, in how many days will the road be finished?

B. 8 days
C. 15 days
D. 6 days
Answer: 10 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_negative_positive_mixed_work
Explanation:
Choose work units so that all rates stay comparable.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Apply the active rates for their respective times.
\[
T=\frac{150}{18-3}
\]
Use the resulting work equation to compute the asked quantity.
\[
T=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 10 days

\]

N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{192}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-8
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 8 workers

Q408. A and B begin a job together. A alone needs 6 days and B alone needs 20 days. A leaves after 2 days. In how many days will the job be completed in all?

Difficulty: Medium

\[

\[

\]

\]

Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{80}{2+6}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+2=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 12 days

---

Q436. Workers build 15 metres of boundary each day, while 5 metres are spoiled daily. In how many days will 120 metres be completed?
A. 12 days
B. 10 days
C. 14 2/5 days
D. 17 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_digging_filling_negative_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
T=\frac{120}{15-5}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q439. A alone finishes a job in 6 days, while B alone finishes it in 10 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?

Answer: 3 3/4 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

\]
Answer: 3 3/4 days

D. 7/8

\[

\]

\[
R=1-\frac{3}{24}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{21}{24}=7/8
\]

\]
Answer: 7/8

Q453. A and B begin a job together. A alone needs 24 days and B alone needs 6 days. A leaves after 4 days. In how many days will the job be completed in all?

\[

\]

\[

\]

\]

\]

T=3+\frac{117}{15}=10.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 10 4/5 days

Q472. A job needs 18 days to finish. After 3 days of work, what part of the job is still left?
A. 5/6
B. 5/1
C. 1/6
D. 6/5
Answer: 5/6
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_residual_work
Explanation:
Convert the given time into the fraction of work completed.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Use the remaining or required fraction of work.
\[
R=1-\frac{3}{18}
\]
Scale the time in the same ratio as the work.
\[
R=\frac{15}{18}=5/6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{18-3}{18}=5/6
\]
Answer: 5/6

Q473. There is enough food for 20 people for 30 days. What is its duration for 25 people?
A. 19 days
B. 24 days
C. 26 days
D. 30 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
20\times 30=25\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{20\times 30}{25}
\]
Answer: 24 days

Q476. A can complete a work in 10 days and B can complete it in 20 days. In the same number of days, what is the ratio of the work done by A and B?
A. 10:20
B. 30:20
C. 2:1
D. 20:30
Answer: 2:1
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_done_ratio_from_times
Explanation:
Convert time or efficiency data into work rates.
\[
W_A:W_B=\frac{1}{10}:\frac{1}{20}
\]
Combine or compare the rates as required.
\[
W_A:W_B=20:10
\]
Convert the final rate relation back into time or ratio.
\[
W_A:W_B=2:1
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=2:1
\]
Answer: 2:1

Q477. A team of 10 workers has completed 60 items from a 240-item order. With 8 days left, how many workers must be added?
A. 8 workers
B. 14 workers
C. 9.75 workers
D. 13 workers
Answer: 13 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_contract_deadline_extra_workers
Explanation:
Total work is proportional to workers x days x hours.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Keep the total work same on both sides of the equation.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{180}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-10
\]
Solve the remaining relation for the required workforce or delay.
\[
N_{extra}=13
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 13 workers
